MEMORANDUM 
 
DATE: 
September 21, 2022 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins 
 
 
Members of the City Council 
 
 
FROM: 
Thomas D. Cobb, PE, City Engineer 
 
 
SUBJECT: 
Railcar Storage Agreement between Cheyenne Rail Hub, LLC and the City 
of Cheyenne  
 
 
Please find the attached proposed Railcar Storage Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Cheyenne Rail Hub, LLC being initiated by the City Engineering and Planning and Development 
Departments.  
 
Reason for Agreement:  
 
The primary reasons for the Railcar Storage Agreement: 
▪ Implementation of the Tourism Master Plan.    
In 2021, Cheyenne approved a new Tourism Master Plan that included the development 
of a new railroad attraction as one of its top goals.  The main objective of that project is 
to showcase Cheyenne’s railroad heritage.   As a part of implementing that plan, the 
Union Pacific Railroad (UPRR) is donating fifteen (15) passenger railcars and a caboose 
which will require temporary storage. 
▪ Temporary storage of up to fifteen (15) rail cars.   
The railcars will be stored in an inconspicuous portion in the northwest portion of the rail 
park not directly visible or accessible to the public.   
▪ Potential rehabilitation and/ or asbestos abatement staging area.   Provide a potential 
area for the purpose of either renovation and/or asbestos abatement of some of the 
railcars.   
 
Cost and Funding: 
 
For each railcar which is stored pursuant to the term of the Agreement, the Storage Facility will 
receive the following fees: 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
A COMMUNITY OF CHOICE 
OFFICE OF THE CITY ENGINEER 
2101 O’Neil Avenue Room 206  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
Phone (307)637-6268    Fax (307)637-6256 

Memorandum 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins 
Members of City Council 
 
September 22, 2022 
Page 2 of 2 
 
▪ Storage of the rail cars will be without cost to the City of Cheyenne for a period of one 
year from the execution of this agreement.  
 
▪ After the one-year period, a ten (10) dollar per railcar, per day for each day railcar or 
fraction thereof is on Storage Facility's tracks or property.  
 
▪ All outside costs incurred from the Class One rail Operator Union Pacific Railroad 
(UPRR), or the Short Line Operator (SRRR), moving into or out of the Storage Facility 
will be passed onto, or paid by the City of Cheyenne. 
 
Therefore, the Summary of Costs and Funding are anticipated as follows: 
 
▪ Costs: 
o 15 railcars x $10/day x 365 days = $54,750/ annually beginning in year two (2). 
o Outside costs incurred from the Class One rail Operator UPRR will be under 
separate agreement. 
▪ Funding:  
o 2017 Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund  
o EDA Grant No. 05-79-06167 Cheyenne Railcar Project Grant (2 Cars) 
 
Contract Term: 
 
The contract is predicated on the storage of railcars and will terminate upon removal of the 
railcars. 

RAILCAR STORAGE AGREEMENT 
This Railcar Storage Agreement “Agreement” dated ____________, between Cheyenne 
Rail Hub, LLC (“Storage Facility”) and the City of Cheyenne, (“City”), individually and 
collectively referred to as “Party” or “Parties”.  The purpose of this Agreement is to provide for 
the temporary storage by Storage Facility of railcars owned, leased, or managed by the City. 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
I. 
STORAGE OF RAILCARS 
A. 
The City desires to store up to fifteen (15) rail cars on the ground near, but 
not on, the Storage Facility’s tracks for the purpose of renovation and 
asbestos abatement.  The City of Cheyenne will be responsible for loading 
and unloading of the rail cars either on or off the tracks or on or off a 
vehicular freight transport.  
B. 
If the City desires to store its railcars on the Storage Facility’s tracks, it will 
notify the Storage Facility of the number of railcars it wishes to store and 
the dates on which the railcars will be tendered for storage.  The Storage 
Facility will promptly advise as to whether, and to what extent, it can 
accommodate the City’s request. 
II. 
FEES 
For each railcar which is stored pursuant to the term of this Agreement, Storage 
Facility will receive the following fees: 
A. 
Storage of the rail cars will be without cost to the City of Cheyenne 
for a period of one year from the execution of this agreement.   
B. 
After the one-year period, a ten (10) dollar per railcar, per day for 
each day railcar or fraction thereof is on Storage Facility's tracks or 
property for the purpose of storage pursuant to this Agreement 
(“Monthly Fee”). 
C. 
All outside costs incurred from the Class One rail Operator Union 
Pacific Railroad (UPRR), or the Short Line Operator (SRRR), 
moving into or out of the Storage Facility will be passed onto, or paid 
by the City of Cheyenne. 
III. 
INVOICING; PAYMENT OF CHARGES 
All fees shall be invoiced monthly by Storage Facility to the City. 
IV. 
FREEDOM FROM LIENS 
Storage Facility covenants and agrees that no claim of lien, statutory or otherwise 
will be made against City's rail cars stored by Storage Facility but that Storage 
Facility will make and collect all charges for storage as an open unsecured account; 
and if there are any claims of lien which might be applied to such storage, this 
By Stefanie Boster at 10:54 am, Sep 21, 2022

 
Railcar Storage Agreement 
Page 2 of 4 
Section shall be construed as a release of any such lien rights. 
V. 
EFFECTIVE DATE AND TERM 
A. 
This Agreement shall become effective as of the date of the last signature 
on this agreement (“Execution Date”) unless sooner terminated or extended 
as provided in this Agreement. Either party may terminate this Agreement 
on thirty (30) days advance written notice to the other party hereunder. 
B. 
If this Agreement is terminated, the City shall, within thirty (30) days or by 
the date of natural expiration of this Agreement, forward Storage Facility 
shall move all of City's railcars stored on Storage Facility’s tracks.  
VI. 
DEGREE OF CARE; LIMITATION OF LIABILITY 
A. 
Storage Facility shall store City’s railcars with the same degree of care that 
it would observe in storing its own railcars and railcars currently or 
previously stored for others.  
B. 
Storage Facility will not be responsible for any loss or damage to any railcar 
or lading therein, stored pursuant to this Agreement, except to the extent 
caused by Storage Facility's negligence or willful misconduct. 
C. 
The City or it’s agent shall maintain and mow the area approximately 25’ 
from the surrounding the Storage Facility where the rail cars are housed for 
the purpose a fire barrier.  
D. 
The City or its contractors shall maintain insurance related to its activities 
or assets.  
VII. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
Storage Facility agrees to indemnify, hold harmless and defend the City from and 
against any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the cost 
and expenses incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may 
hereafter arise as a result the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including 
death or bodily injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property, 
contamination of or adverse effects on the environment, or any violation of 
governmental laws, regulations or orders to the extent caused by (1) the 
Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) any 
negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or 
sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant 
acknowledges that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the 
terms of this paragraph. 
VIII. NOTICE 
All notices required to be given by either party to the other (unless expressly stated 
otherwise in this Agreement), must be in writing and shall be delivered to the other 
party by overnight messenger service, United States certified mail with return 
receipt requested, or by overnight courier, and addressed as shown below. Either

IX. 
SEVERABLIITY 
In the event that any provision of this Agreement (or portion thereof) is determined 
by a court of competent jurisdiction to be invalid or otherwise unenforceable, such 
provision (or part thereof) shall be enforced to the extent possible consistent within 
the stated intention of the parties, or if incapable of such enforcement, shall be 
deemed to be deleted from this Agreement, while the remainder of this Agreement 
shall continue in full force and remain in effect according to its stated terms and 
conditions. 
X. 
ASSIGNMENT 
This Agreement may not be assigned by either party without the prior written 
consent of the other. 
XI. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Agreement shall be 
governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming 
shall have jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the

XII. GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent 
necessary for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written 
terms of the Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities 
and defenses available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. 
Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations 
of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be 
constructed as a waiver of governmental immunity. The parties agree that any 
ambiguity in this Agreement shall not be strictly construed, either against or for 
either party, except that any ambiguity as to governmental or sovereign immunity 
shall be construed in favor of governmental immunity. 
XIII. ENTIRE AGREEMENT 
This Agreement sets forth the entire agreement between the Parties, and replaces 
and supersedes any prior agreements, understandings, negotiations, or discussions

MEMORANDUM 
 
DATE: 
September 21, 2022 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins 
 
 
Members of the City Council 
 
 
FROM: 
Thomas D. Cobb, PE, City Engineer 
 
 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement for the 
Reed Avenue Rail Corridor Scoping and Railroad Negotiation Project  
 
 
Recently, the City of Cheyenne has found it difficult to engage and find a design concept 
acceptable to the Burlington Northern Santa Fe Railroad on an economic revitalization project in 
our West Edge District.  This project is known as the Reed Avenue Rail Corridor Project. More 
specifically, the project is located along a seldomly used rail corridor that has been declining 
since trucking became the preferred method of freight movement in the area. 
 
The City of Cheyenne would like to engage a firm that has experience with negotiation and 
designing public pedestrian components and improvements within and adjacent to Class I rails 
and rail yards.   
 
The Engineering staff including Wes Bay, PE reviewed the Statement of Qualifications on file 
with the City of Cheyenne and found Quandel’s qualifications, personnel, and expertise would 
provide us with leadership, program management, design capabilities, and ability to negotiate 
our position with the Burlington Norther Santa Fe required to move this important project 
forward.  This phase of the project would then provide the basis for the final design and eventual 
construction project.  
 
Reason for Agreement:  
 
▪ Project program management and negotiation with the BNSF,  
▪ Develop of the concept plan(s) in concurrence with the BNSF,  
▪ Public Involvement (re-engage project stakeholders), 
▪ Resolution of right-of-way and easement issues; and lastly  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
A COMMUNITY OF CHOICE 
OFFICE OF THE CITY ENGINEER 
2101 O’Neil Avenue Room 206  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
Phone (307)637-6268    Fax (307)637-6256 

Memorandum 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins 
Members of City Council 
 
September 21, 2022 
Page 2 of 2 
 
▪ Researching and finding additional funding opportunities for the final design and 
construction. 
 
Cost and Funding: 
 
Funding for this project will come from the 2017 1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax. The 
total professional services will be a cost not-to-exceed $161,605.00. We respectfully request 
your approval of this professional services agreement. 
 
 
Contract Term: 
 
The contract time is until completion.  However, the project schedule developed by the 
consultant anticipates completion by February, 2023.   

City Contract No. ____________

provisions. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount

($161,605.00).  
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the

not meeting such a standard. 
 
5.

in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances.

DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and 
sub-consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees, or 
sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated.

to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring 
experts and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in 
progress; to examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this 
Agreement; and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 
 
20. 
OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT 
All deliverables by the Consultant, all work product of the Consultant, all notes, 
calculations, memoranda, or any other documentation generated by the Consultant 
pursuant to the scope of this Agreement, shall be the property of the City of Cheyenne 
and shall be provided to the City of Cheyenne immediately upon request. 
 
21. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age 
Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, 
and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, 
race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection 
with the performance under this Agreement. 
 
22. 
FORCE MAJEURE. 
The performance of the Agreement by either party shall be subject to force majeure 
including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural disaster, war or threat of 
war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental 
regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by the World Health 
Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government authority or health 
agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-19, H1N1, or similar 
infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other similar occurrence 
beyond the control of the parties, where any of those factors, circumstances, situations, or 
conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or unreasonably delay the performance 
required by this Agreement. The Agreement may be cancelled by either party, without 
liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or amounts paid shall be 
refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written notice to the other party. 
 
 
 

 
Professional Services Agreement 
For Reed Avenue Rail Corridor Scoping and Railroad Negotiation Project 
Page 7 of 7 
23. 
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION 
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this 
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing 
that he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary. 
 
24. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement consisting of seven (7) pages and Exhibit A consisting of one (1) page 
contain the entire understanding of the parties. There are no other terms or conditions, 
written or oral, concerning or controlling this matter.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-09-26_TotalExtraction_44066507.pdf
 
LEASE AGREEMENT 
 
 
This Lease is made this _____ day of ________________, 2022, between the City of 
Cheyenne, a municipal corporation organized under the laws of the State of Wyoming, 2101 
O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 (“City”), and the Lamar Advertising of South Dakota, 
LLC dba Lamar Advertising of Casper, tradename: The Lamar Companies, a limited liability 
company organized and existing under the laws of the State of South Dakota, 5321 Corporate 
Blvd., Baton Rouge, Louisiana 70808 (“Lessee”). 
 
1. 
Description of Property; Term. 
 
 
In consideration of the rent provided for in Section Two of this Lease, City leases to Lessee 
real property belonging to City located in Laramie County, Wyoming, and more particularly 
described as a site for the placement of three billboard advertising signs described as billboard 
238-7566 measuring not more than 40 feet in length by 10 feet in height located approximately 
15.5 miles West of Junction I-25 and I-80 on the South side of I-80; billboard 238-7567 measuring 
not more than 28 feet in length by 10 feet in height located approximately 16 miles West of 
Junction I-25 and I-80 on the South side of I-80; and billboard 238-7568 measuring not more than 
30 feet in length and 10 feet in height located approximately 2.7 miles West of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, for a term of five (5) years beginning on February 26, 2023.  That date shall be 
hereinafter referred to as the “commencement date.”  The advertising sign site is hereinafter 
referred to as the “leased property.” 
 
2. 
Rent 
 
 
Lessee shall pay City an annual rent of One Thousand Eight Hundred Fifty Dollars 
($1850.00), including $750 for billboard 238-7566, $500 for billboard 238-7567 and $600 for 
billboard 238-7568, payable within thirty (30) days after the commencement date, and payable in 
advance on the commencement date in each year thereafter for the entire term of the Lease. 
 
3. 
Use of Property 
 
Lessee shall use the leased property for the construction and maintenance of three 
billboards as described in Section One above, and for no other purpose whatsoever.  Lessee shall 
not place on the leased property any advertisement for alcohol or tobacco products. 
 
4. 
Compliance with the Law 
 
 
In its use of the leased property, Lessee shall fully comply with all applicable federal, state, 
county and municipal statues, rules, regulations, and ordinances.  In its use of the leased property, 
Lessee shall secure from the proper authorities all required licenses and permits before 
commencing any construction, re-construction, or significant repair of the billboard. 
 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 4:23 pm, Aug 17, 2022

 
Lamar Billboard Lease 
Page 2 of 5 
5. 
Repairs and Maintenance 
 
Lessee shall, at all times during the term of this Lease, keep all improvements placed by 
Lessee on the leased property in good repair, and shall maintain them at all times in a clean and 
attractive condition.  Construction and maintenance activities shall be conducted in a manner that 
is not hazardous to livestock, including the installation of fencing if necessary to prevent 
unreasonable risks of harm to livestock.  Lessee shall be responsible for all livestock injuries and 
deaths caused or in any way resulting from construction or maintenance activities. 
 
6. 
Insurance Requirement 
 
 
Lessee shall obtain and keep in full force and effect at all times during the term of this 
Lease, the following specified insurance coverages for the protection of City and Lessee against 
liability arising out of the condition of the leased property, the improvements erected on the 
property, or any operations of Lessee conducted pursuant to this Lease.  In the event Lessee retains 
independent contractors to perform any operations on City land pursuant to this Lease, the 
insurance requirements stated in this Section shall also apply to each and every such contractor. 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of 
$1,000,000.00 per occurrence, $2,000,000.00 aggregate for the entire term of this Lease. 
 
Business Automobile Insurance 
Including owned, non-owned and hired vehicles with minimum limits for bodily injury 
and property damage of $1,000,000.00 per accident for the entire term of this Lease. 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ Compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Lease, as 
required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Lease.  Lessee shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or employer’s 
liability insurance coverage. 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
Lessee shall provide the City copies of certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to entering upon City’s land for the purpose of conducting 
any operations pursuant to this Lease. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory.  All 
policies required under this Lease shall be in effect for the duration of this Lease.  It shall 
be an affirmative obligation upon Lessee to immediately notify the City’s Risk Manager, 
City Clerk, and City Attorney of any fact, circumstances, or occurrence that has resulted 
in or may result in the cancellation or substantive change of any insurance coverage 
required by this Lease, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach of this Lease. 
 

 
Lamar Billboard Lease 
Page 3 of 5 
In addition, Lessee shall provide the City with copies of insurance policies and policy 
endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  The City’s failure to 
request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall not affect the City’s 
rights or Lessee’s obligations hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Lease shall have a minimum A. 
M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
7. 
Ownership Improvements 
 
 
All improvements erected by Lessee on the leased property shall remain the property of 
Lessee and shall be removed by Lessee at the termination of this Lease or any renewal of the Lease. 
 
8. 
Access to Leased Property 
 
 
City grants Lessee the right to pass to and from the leased property over City’s private 
roads located on the City’s Belvoir Ranch for the purposes of construction, maintenance and repair 
of the billboard advertising sign located on the leased premises; provided, however that Lessee 
shall use said private roads in accordance with good management practices and shall take 
reasonable precautions to prevent damage to said roads and to the ranch lands adjacent to said 
roads and the leased property. 
 
9. 
Termination 
 
A. 
By City. 
 
1. 
If the City should make a bona fide contract for the sale of the leased 
property, the City may, at City’s option, terminate this Lease by 
giving Lessee one hundred and eighty (180) days’ notice in writing, 
and refunding to Lessee the rental paid in advance pro rata for the 
unexpired term of this Lease. 
 
2. 
If the Lessee defaults in the payment of rent or breaches any other 
covenant or condition of this Lease, the City may, in addition to any 
other remedy that the City may have, declare forfeiture of this Lease 
for the default, re-enter the leased property, and remove all personal 
property from the premises. 
 
B. 
By Lessee.  During the term of this Lease, should the use of the leased 
property for the purpose permitted by this Lease be prohibited, limited, or 
restricted by the terms of any restriction, covenant, regulation, ordinance, 
or zoning resolution affecting the property, Lessee may, at Lessee’s option 
terminate this Lease by giving the City thirty (30) days’ notice in writing of 
its election to do so. 
 

 
Lamar Billboard Lease 
Page 4 of 5 
C. 
Upon termination of this Lease, Lessee shall restore the leased property to 
its natural state including, without limitation, filling all holes and removing 
all debris caused by the removal of billboard signs and other items of 
personal property placed on the leased property by Lessee. 
 
10. 
Effect of Condemnation 
 
 
If the leased property, or any part of the leased property, is taken by eminent domain, this 
Lease shall terminate on the date when title vests pursuant to such taking. 
 
11. 
Renewal Option 
 
 
The Lessee shall have the option to extend this Lease for one additional five (5) year period, 
provided, however, that said option may be exercised only if (1) the Lessee is not in default of any 
of the terms and conditions of this Lease at the time the option is exercised; (2) the Lessee has 
practiced good stewardship of the leased property, access roads, and adjacent ranch lands as 
determined by the City in the exercise of its reasonable discretion; and (3) the Lessee provides 
written notice to the City of its intent to exercise its option to renew at least one hundred and twenty  
(120) days prior to the scheduled expiration of this Lease, unless such terms and conditions are 
amended or renegotiated pursuant to mutual written agreement. 
 
12. 
Indemnification 
 
 
The Lessee agrees to defend, hold harmless, and indemnify the City of Cheyenne, its 
officials, employees, agents, and authorized volunteers against all claims and costs, including 
attorney’s fees, arising during, or resulting from the Lessee’s performance of its obligations under 
this Lease.  The Lessee acknowledges its understanding of this paragraph and realizes it may have 
a financial responsibility to the City.  The City does not waive any applicable defenses and 
expressly reserves the right to invoke governmental immunity pursuant to the Wyoming 
Governmental Claims Act, Wyo. Stat. 1-39-101, et seq. for any claim arising out of this Lease. 
 
 
It is expressly understood and agreed that although the City has the right to observe and 
review the work and operations of the Lessee, this shall not relieve the Lessee from any of its 
covenants and obligations hereunder, and the Lessee shall be responsible for and hold the City and 
its officers, employees, agents and representatives harmless from all suits, actions or claims of any 
character due to injuries or damages sustained by any person or property in consequence of any 
breach or of default under this Lease, or of the failure to observe safety standards or regulations, 
or otherwise, or through the use of unsafe or unacceptable practices or materials in the performance 
of any operations under this Lease, or the Lessee’s failure to comply with any law, ordinance or 
regulation. 
 
The parties have executed this Lease the day and year first above written. 
 
 
 
 

 
Lamar Billboard Lease

year first above written.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-09-26_TotalExtraction_20683803.pdf
CITY OF CHEYENNE 
CONTRACT MODIFICATION 
 
 
Contract Modification: 
#1 
Project: 
 
 
Audit the Annual Financial Statements of the City of Cheyenne 
and its Board of Public Utilities 
Project Number: 
 
Contract #7488 
Contractor: 
 
 
McGee, Hearne & Paiz (MHP) LLP 
Date Prepared: 
 
August 31, 2022 
 
The above referenced contract is amended or modified as set forth in the following and these 
amendments and modifications of the plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All other 
aspects of the original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect. 
 
I. Description of Changes 
 
Per the Single Audit Act, governmental recipients of $750,000 or more per year in federal financial 
assistance must undergo an organization-wide financial and compliance audit.  When MHP provided 
the cost proposal for the City’s annual single audit for Fiscal Year 2022, the total amount of federal 
expenditures from July 1, 2021 to June 30, 2022 was not known.  Once the City Treasurer prepared 
the Schedule of Expenditures for Federal Awards (SEFA) and provided it to MHP, they determined 
that a total of seven federal programs were required to be audited instead of the four that were done 
the previous fiscal year. In Fiscal Year 2021, the City had $8,273,134 in federal expenditures and 36 
federal grant awards.  In comparison, in Fiscal Year 2022 there were 43 awards, an increase of 
seven.  Additionally, federal expenditures increased by $3,445,558 to $11,718,692 in Fiscal Year 
2022.  Therefore, MHP is requesting a contract modification in the amount of $10,500, which is 
$3,500 for each additional federal program.  
 
II. New Contract Items 
 
Audit of three additional federal programs that were not included in the original scope of the project. 
This Contract Modification #1 also includes Federal Provisions and Clauses, as required by the 
funding source (American Rescue Plan Federal Funds). 
 
III. Schedule 
 
This Contract Modification #1 will not have an impact to the project schedule.  The annual audit 
will be conducted as previously scheduled. 
 
IV. Summary 
Original Contract Amount ...................................................................................$71,125.00 
Previous Modifications ......................................................................................................$0 
Contract Amount Prior to this Modification ........................................................$71,125.00 
This Modification ................................................................................................$10,500.00 
New Contract Amount .........................................................................................$81,625.00 
By Stefanie Boster at 6:50 pm, Sep 06, 2022

APPENDIX II TO PART 200 - CONTRACT PROVISIONS FOR NON-FEDERAL 
ENTITY CONTRACTS UNDER FEDERAL AWARDS  
In addition to other provisions required by the Federal agency or non-Federal entity, all contracts 
made by the non-Federal entity under the Federal award must contain provisions covering the 
following, as applicable.  
(A) Contracts for more than the simplified acquisition threshold, which is the inflation adjusted 
amount determined by the Civilian Agency Acquisition Council and the Defense Acquisition 
Regulations Council (Councils) as authorized by 41 U.S.C. 1908, must address administrative, 
contractual, or legal remedies in instances where contractors violate or breach contract terms, and 
provide for such sanctions and penalties as appropriate.  
(B) All contracts in excess of $10,000 must address termination for cause and for convenience by 
the non-Federal entity including the manner by which it will be effected and the basis for 
settlement.  
(G) Clean Air Act (42 U.S.C. 7401-7671q.) and the Federal Water Pollution Control Act (33 
U.S.C. 1251-1387), as amended - Contracts and subgrants of amounts in excess of $150,000 must 
contain a provision that requires the non-Federal award to agree to comply with all applicable 
standards, orders or regulations issued pursuant to the Clean Air Act (42 U.S.C. 7401-7671q) and 
the Federal Water Pollution Control Act as amended (33 U.S.C. 1251-1387). Violations must be 
reported to the Federal awarding agency and the Regional Office of the Environmental Protection 
Agency (EPA).  
Environmental Policy Acts.  Contractor agrees all activities under this Contract will comply 
with the Clean Air Act, the Clean Water Act, the National Environmental Policy Act, and 
other related provisions of federal environmental protection laws, rules or regulations. 
(H) Debarment and Suspension (Executive Orders 12549 and 12689) - A contract award (see 2 
CFR 180.220) must not be made to parties listed on the governmentwide exclusions in the System 
for Award Management (SAM), in accordance with the OMB guidelines at 2 CFR 180 that 
implement Executive Orders 12549 (3 CFR part 1986 Comp., p. 189) and 12689 (3 CFR part 1989 
Comp., p. 235), “Debarment and Suspension.” SAM Exclusions contains the names of parties 
debarred, suspended, or otherwise excluded by agencies, as well as parties declared ineligible 
under statutory or regulatory authority other than Executive Order 12549.  
Suspension and Debarment.  By signing this Contract, Contractor certifies that neither it 
nor its principals/agents are presently debarred, suspended, proposed for debarment, declared 
ineligible, or voluntarily excluded from participation in this transaction or from receiving 
federal financial or nonfinancial assistance, nor are any of the participants involved in the 
execution of this Contract suspended, debarred, or voluntarily excluded by any federal 
department or agency in accordance with Executive Order 12549 (Debarment and 
Suspension), 44 CFR  Part 17, or 2 CFR Part 180, or are on the debarred, or otherwise 
ineligible, vendors lists maintained by the federal government.  Further, Contractor agrees to 
notify Agency by certified mail should it or any of its principals/agents become ineligible for 
payment, debarred, suspended, or voluntarily excluded from receiving federal funds during 
the term of this Contract. 
(I) Byrd Anti-Lobbying Amendment (31 U.S.C. 1352) - Contractors that apply or bid for an award 
exceeding $100,000 must file the required certification. Each tier certifies to the tier above that it 
will not and has not used Federal appropriated funds to pay any person or organization for 
influencing or attempting to influence an officer or employee of any agency, a member of 

Congress, officer or employee of Congress, or an employee of a member of Congress in 
connection with obtaining any Federal contract, grant or any other award covered by 31 U.S.C.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-09-26_TotalExtraction_41913316.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd 
Subject: Big Hole Cooperative Management Plan  
Date: 
September 26, 2022 
 
The item for consideration is an update to the Big Hole Cooperative Management Plan as originally 
approved in 2016. The original contract calls for a review to occur every 5 years, and in this review, 
some elements of the original contract were identified for changes.    
 
The following is a brief summary of the proposed changes: 
1. The allowance of powered devices for handicapped individuals (Section II, Subsection 8, part I 
and part J.3). 
2. Minor formatting changes. 
3. Updated signature block. 
 
The Exhibits to this document include the original contract and associated exhibits. No changes to the 
exhibits as originally adopted are proposed. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

Cooperative Land Management Plan 
Red Mountain Ranch Wyoming - The Big Hole 
The City of Cheyenne 
Larimer County Open Lands Program 
The Nature Conservancy 
Date:_________________________ 
 
I. Recitals: Background Information 
 
1. 
This Cooperative Land Management Plan (“Cooperative Plan”) is made and 
entered into by and between The Nature Conservancy, a non-profit corporation organized under 
the laws of the District of Columbia (the “Conservancy”), the City of Cheyenne (the “City”), a 
municipal corporation organized under the laws of the State of Wyoming, and the County of 
Larimer, Colorado (“Larimer County”), a county government organized under the laws of the State 
of Colorado.   
 
2. 
This Cooperative Plan applies to a tract of land consisting of approximately 1,800 
acres, known generally as the Laramie Foothills Red Mountain Ranch—Wyoming (the 
“Property”), also known as the “Big Hole.”  
 
3. 
The Conservancy reserved a conservation easement (the “Easement”) on the 
Property when the Property was conveyed to the City on July 12th, 2005. The Easement is attached 
as Exhibit 1. 
 
4. 
The Easement stipulates that a cooperative management plan will be developed and 
adopted by the Conservancy, the City and Larimer County (collectively, the “Parties”), referred to 
in the Easement as the “Cooperative Plan.” The Cooperative Plan, currently in effect, was executed 
by the Parties on January 19, 2017 and is attached as Exhibit 2. This Cooperative Plan will replace 
and supersede the January 19, 2017 Cooperative Plan in its entirety.  
 
5. 
The Property currently remains in a substantially undisturbed, natural state, has 
significant ecological and open-space values, and provides relatively significant natural habitat for 
native plants and wildlife.  Protection of the Property through the conservation easement will 
contribute to the conservation of important relatively significant natural habitat for plants, 
including particularly native foothills grasslands, ponderosa pine forest, mountain mahogany, 
skunkbush sumac and rabbit brush shrub lands, and for wildlife, including elk, deer, antelope, 
nesting raptors, migratory and breeding birds and mammals (the “Conservation Values”).  In 
addition, the Property harbors scenic and agricultural values worthy of protection.  The Property 
lies adjacent to the Red Mountain Ranch in Colorado, a property owned and managed as open 
space by Larimer County.  This Plan provides for cooperative and compatible management 
across the boundary line between the states of Wyoming and Colorado to facilitate landscape-scale 
conservation.  All the natural elements and ecological values are worthy of conservation and are 
of great importance to City, the Conservancy, and Larimer County. 
6. 
The intention of the Easement is to preserve and protect the Property in perpetuity 
and, in the event of their degradation or destruction, to enhance and restore the open space and 
relatively significant natural features and values of the Property.  The Easement also furthers the 
By Stefanie Boster at 12:20 am, Sep 21, 2022

specific purpose of conserving important habitat for wildlife, protecting rare and unique native 
plants currently known and later identified, conserving the diverse forest, grassland, riparian and 
vegetative communities, and protecting the wildlife inhabiting these communities.  The intention 
of the Cooperative Plan as stipulated in the Easement is to permit the continuation of such uses of 
the Property as may now be conducted thereon consistent with the Conservation Values protected 
herein. 
 
7. 
The Property shall be operated and managed in accordance with this Cooperative 
Plan, which shall include but not be limited to guidelines for appropriate land stewardship, 
recreational uses, and public access.  This Cooperative Plan shall be reviewed and revised at least 
once every five (5) years.  
 
II. Public Access 
 
8. 
One of the principal goals of this Cooperative Plan is to establish access for Public 
Use and enjoyment of the Property consistent with the Easement.    
 
A. 
Public access is allowed on the Cheyenne Rim Trail, from trailheads initiating 
in Colorado in the Red Mountain Open Space.  Activities currently allowed 
on the Cheyenne Rim Trail include horseback riding, hiking and non-motorized 
biking.  Visitors must stay “on trail” and the existing trail shall be maintained 
and patrolled by Larimer County staff.   
 
It is anticipated that public access trail connectors will be established in the 
future from the Cheyenne Rim Trail to the Robert E. Green Trail and to the 
Belvoir Ranch.  Public access provisions and restrictions will be established 
when the proposed Robert E. Green and Belvoir Trail connectors are 
constructed and shall be compatible with existing public access in Colorado and 
those established for the Property by the Easement.  
 
B. 
No dogs are allowed on the Property, except for service dogs, as defined 
pursuant to the Americans with Disabilities Act.  All service dogs must be 
harnessed, leashed, or tethered, unless these devices interfere with the service 
animal's work or an individual's disability prevents the individual from using 
these devices.  If a service dog is not harnessed, leashed, or tethered, the 
individual must maintain control of the service dog through voice, signal, or 
other effective controls. 
 
C. 
No camping or fires of any kind are permitted on the Property. 
 
D. 
No hunting or fishing is permitted on the Property, except by special permit 
from the City. 
 
E. 
No trapping is permitted on the Property, except as may be permitted pursuant 
to the conditions specified in the Easement Documentation Report (“EDR”).  
The EDR is attached as Exhibit 3. 
 

F. 
No archeological, paleontological, or cultural artifacts may be removed from 
the Property, unless expressly permitted in writing by the Conservancy. 
 
G. 
No rocks or plants may be removed from the Property, unless expressly 
permitted in writing by the Conservancy. 
 
H. 
No public access to the Property is permitted from December through February.  
The Property is closed seasonally due to sensitive wintering range for large 
mammals.   
 
I. 
No public motorized access to the Property is permitted, except for visitors with 
mobility challenges who may require the use of other power-driven mobility 
devices, or with the express written consent of the City and the Conservancy.    
 
J. 
The City, the County and the Conservancy each may guide public engagement 
tours for educational programs, natural history interpretation, resource 
management and scientific research, provided that:  
 
1. 
All activity is limited to the existing roads and trails, as documented in 
the attached map provided in Exhibit 4. 
 
2. 
At least one staff person from one of the parties to this Plan or a 
designated and trained representative of the group shall be present and 
responsible for the group. 
 
3. 
Use of motorized vehicles is limited to existing roads, or trails where 
other power-driven mobility devices are necessary and compatible, on 
the Property. 
 
4. 
If conditions are such that motorized or other forms of access may 
negatively impact roads, such as during wet conditions, trips must be 
postponed or rescheduled. 
 
5. 
To facilitate visitation and minimize impacts to the Conservation 
Values, each individual and group using motorized access shall stop, 
park, and turn around on the existing two-track roads as indicated on the 
map attached as Exhibit 4.  Every effort shall be made to minimize 
impacts to the resource.  
 
6. 
If impacts to the Property’s Conservation Values occur, an appropriate 
stopping site for tours will be cooperatively established between the 
Parties.  In accordance with the Easement, the stopping site shall be 
temporary, shall not be paved, oiled, or improved in any way, and shall 
be, to the greatest extent practicable, restored to its condition prior to its 
temporary use. 
 
 

7. 
It is understood that uncontrolled visits by tour groups may be 
detrimental to the Conservation Values protected by the Easement.  
Restrictions of the frequency of group visits and the number of persons 
in each group may be necessary to control adverse impacts on the 
Conservation Values protected by the Easement.  The need for 
additional use restrictions will be considered after each annual review 
of the Property (usually late summer) by the Conservancy site 
monitoring team in collaboration with the City and Larimer County 
officials.  Restrictions may be imposed from year to year or for longer 
terms at the discretion of the Parties.   
 
III. County, City, Conservancy Access to Property 
 
9. 
One of the principal goals of this Cooperative Plan is to establish guidelines for 
access to the Property by the Parties to this Cooperative Plan in accordance with the Easement.  
 
A. 
There are four access agreements affecting the Property, attached as Exhibits 4, 
5, 6, and 7.  The City will grant access (either motorized or foot) to the County 
across an existing road, as shown on Exhibit 4, so that the County can access a 
portion of its Colorado property that is inaccessible from Colorado.  The 
access is for emergency, property management, and patrol purposes. 
 
B. 
The County will grant access (either motorized or foot) to the Conservancy 
across the Red Mountain Open Space Colorado property to enable the 
Conservancy to access the Property to monitor and enforce the terms of the 
Easement.  
 
C. 
Access to the Property by the Conservancy is granted by the City pursuant to 
the Easement.  
 
D. 
The City and the Conservancy will have access (either motorized or foot) to the 
Property through the proposed Robert E. Green Public Access Agreement 
entered into between the City, Robert E. Green, and the Conservancy that will 
provide for the construction of public access trails on the west side of the 
Property.  
 
E. 
The County will maintain and patrol the Cheyenne Rim Trail and will post non-
illuminated signage reminding visitors that they must stay “on trail” and 
advising users that access to the Property north of the Wyoming state line is 
prohibited.  It is anticipated that a trail connector will be established pursuant 
to an access agreement described in sub-section D of this Section, from the 
Cheyenne Rim Trail to the Robert E. Green Property and the Belvoir Ranch.  
Public access provisions and restrictions will be established when the proposed 
Robert E. Green and Belvoir Trail connectors are constructed and shall be 
compatible with existing public access in Colorado and those established by the 
Easement.  The Parties to this Cooperative Plan may enter into such other and 
further agreements as may be necessary to define the maintenance and patrol 
responsibilities that will be assumed by the Parties.   

 
E. 
The City, the County and any others who may become parties to future access 
agreements may access the Property in accordance with the terms and 
conditions of such existing and future access agreements.  Existing access 
agreements are intended to implement emergency and management purposes, 
to permit scientific surveys and research and to allow other activities that 
promote the development and maintenance of this Cooperative Plan, subject to 
the terms and conditions of the Easement.  The general public shall not be 
designated as a party in any of the existing or planned access agreements.  
 
IV. Land Management 
 
10. 
One of the principal goals of this Cooperative Plan is to define land management 
activities consistent with the Easement and prioritize current land management issues.  This 
Cooperative Plan is not intended to direct general land management decisions on the Property.  
The overarching goal of land management activities is to conserve the Conservation Values as 
described in the EDR and Easement while facilitating public access.  Based on this goal, the 
following land management activities will be allowed by the owner of the Property: 
 
A. 
Grazing of domestic livestock. 
 
B. 
Grazing and pasturing horses, cattle, mules, sheep, and llamas, provided that 
range conditions shall be maintained at, or improved from, those documented 
in the EDR.  Grazing of other livestock species shall be subject to prior 
approval by the Conservancy. 
 
C. 
The Parties acknowledge that given the topography, historic ownership and 
management of the Property, grazing management has occurred across the 
boundary between the states of Colorado and Wyoming.  Further, the Parties 
agree that grazing pastures on the Property shall be defined by existing fences, 
and not by Property ownership.  Properly managed grazing is expected to have 
no significant impact on the Conservation Values and in some instances is 
necessary to maintain said values.  In addition, grazing practices on the 
adjacent Red Mountain Open Space Property shall also occur in a manner such 
that it maintains range condition in a “good” or “excellent” condition as defined 
by the Natural Resources Conservation Service. 
 
D. 
Fence Maintenance and Repair.  The City shall be responsible for 
maintenance, repair, and reconstruction of existing fencing, as documented in 
the EDR, and for the construction of new fences, provided that boundary or 
pasture-division fences shall not unduly restrict or exclude wildlife use of the 
Property. Wildlife friendly fences are defined by research and various agencies. 
Specifications are available in various textbooks, agency publications, and from  
 
 
 
 
 

the Colorado State University (CSU) and University of Wyoming (UW) 
extension service and NRCS.  These fences facilitate the safe and 
uninterrupted movement of pronghorn, mule deer, and elk.  
 
E. 
Control of Noxious Weeds.  Use of agricultural chemicals for the following 
purposes and under the following conditions are permitted: 
 
1. 
For the control of noxious weeds, as required by Wyoming state law, 
and for the control of other invasive exotic plant species, provided 
that chemical herbicides may be used only in those amounts and 
with a frequency of application that constitute the minimum 
necessary for control, and that the herbicide is not applied by aerial 
spraying. 
 
2. 
For the control of agricultural or forest pests, subject to prior 
approval by the Conservancy.  
 
3. 
Use of biological weed and insect control agents is subject to prior 
approval by the Conservancy. 
 
4. 
For most noxious weeds, chemical treatment is the only effective or 
feasible means of control.  Therefore, chemicals should be allowed 
for use on the Property to treat noxious weeds when appropriate as 
long as used as directed on the labels and in consideration with the 
identified Conservation Values.  Eradication and control of 
noxious weeds are considered essential steps in successful 
conservation. 
 
F. 
Timber Harvest.  The selective harvesting of timber from the Property may be 
allowed but only for the purposes of controlling forest diseases, protecting 
persons or property from the hazards of falling trees or branches, reducing the 
risk of wildfire; and enhancing wildlife habitat.  These conditions apply to 
both living and standing dead trees. 
V. Miscellaneous Provisions 
 
11.  
Amendment: This Plan may be amended by the Parties at any time pursuant to a 
written instrument duly executed by authorized representatives of the Parties.  
 
12.  
The terms and conditions of this Cooperative Plan shall at all times be interpreted 
and implemented according to the overarching goals and objectives, terms and conditions of the 
Easement.  If there are any inconsistencies or conflicts between the provisions contained in this 
Cooperative Plan and the Easement, the terms of the Easement shall prevail.  
 
13.  
Five Year Reviews: This Plan shall be reviewed by the Parties at least once during 
the five (5) years following its execution for the purposes of updates and revisions and at least 
once during each additional five (5) year renewal term.  The Conservancy as holder of the 
 
 

Easement shall call all meetings necessary to review this Plan, set the agenda for each such meeting 
and act in the capacity of chair of the meeting.  In the absence of a call for a meeting by the 
Conservancy, each Party may, upon the five (5) year anniversary of the execution of this Plan, call 
for a meeting to review this Plan, which meeting shall be held at the earliest convenience of the 
Parties hereto.    
 
THE NATURE CONSERVANCY, a District of 
Columbia non-profit corporation, 
 
By: ______________________________ 
 
Its:  _________________________ 
 
Date:    
 
 
State of Wyoming 
) 
 
 
 
)ss 
County of Laramie 
)

___________________________________

THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING

Patrick Collins, Mayor 
 
(SEAL)

LARIMER COUNTY, COLORADO,

)

5. 
To facilitate visitation and minimize impacts to the Conservation 
Values, each individual and group using motorized access shall stop, 
park, and turn around on the existing two-track roads as indicated on the 
map attached as Exhibit 4.  Every effort shall be made to minimize 
impacts to the resource.  
 
6. 
If impacts to the Property’s Conservation Values occur, an appropriate 
stopping site for tours will be cooperatively established between the 
Parties.  In accordance with the Easement, the stopping site shall be 
temporary, shall not be paved, oiled, or improved in any way, and shall 
be, to the greatest extent practicable, restored to its condition prior to its 
temporary use. 
 
 

7. 
It is understood that uncontrolled visits by tour groups may be 
detrimental to the Conservation Values protected by the Easement.  
Restrictions of the frequency of group visits and the number of persons 
in each group may be necessary to control adverse impacts on the 
Conservation Values protected by the Easement.  The need for 
additional use restrictions will be considered after each annual review 
of the Property (usually late summer) by the Conservancy site 
monitoring team in collaboration with the City and Larimer County 
officials.  Restrictions may be imposed from year to year or for longer 
terms at the discretion of the Parties.   
 
III. County, City, Conservancy Access to Property 
 
9. 
One of the principal goals of this Cooperative Plan is to establish guidelines for 
access to the Property by the Parties to this Cooperative Plan in accordance with the Easement.  
 
A. 
There are four access agreements affecting the Property, attached as Exhibits 4, 
5, 6, and 7.  The City will grant access (either motorized or foot) to the County 
across an existing road, as shown on Exhibit 4, so that the County can access a 
portion of its Colorado property that is inaccessible from Colorado.  The 
access is for emergency, property management, and patrol purposes. 
 
B. 
The County will grant access (either motorized or foot) to the Conservancy 
across the Red Mountain Open Space Colorado property to enable the 
Conservancy to access the Property to monitor and enforce the terms of the 
Easement.  
 
C. 
Access to the Property by the Conservancy is granted by the City pursuant to 
the Easement.  
 
D. 
The City and the Conservancy will have access (either motorized or foot) to the 
Property through the proposed Robert E. Green Public Access Agreement 
entered into between the City, Robert E. Green, and the Conservancy that will 
provide for the construction of public access trails on the west side of the 
Property.  
 
E. 
The County will maintain and patrol the Cheyenne Rim Trail and will post non-
illuminated signage reminding visitors that they must stay “on trail” and 
advising users that access to the Property north of the Wyoming state line is 
prohibited.  It is anticipated that a trail connector will be established pursuant 
to an access agreement described in sub-section D of this Section, from the 
Cheyenne Rim Trail to the Robert E. Green Property and the Belvoir Ranch.  
Public access provisions and restrictions will be established when the proposed 
Robert E. Green and Belvoir Trail connectors are constructed and shall be 
compatible with existing public access in Colorado and those established by the 
Easement.  The Parties to this Cooperative Plan may enter into such other and 
further agreements as may be necessary to define the maintenance and patrol 
responsibilities that will be assumed by the Parties.   

 
E. 
The City, the County and any others who may become parties to future access 
agreements may access the Property in accordance with the terms and 
conditions of such existing and future access agreements.  Existing access 
agreements are intended to implement emergency and management purposes, 
to permit scientific surveys and research and to allow other activities that 
promote the development and maintenance of this Cooperative Plan, subject to 
the terms and conditions of the Easement.  The general public shall not be 
designated as a party in any of the existing or planned access agreements.  
 
IV. Land Management 
 
10. 
One of the principal goals of this Cooperative Plan is to define land management 
activities consistent with the Easement and prioritize current land management issues.  This 
Cooperative Plan is not intended to direct general land management decisions on the Property.  
The overarching goal of land management activities is to conserve the Conservation Values as 
described in the EDR and Easement while facilitating public access.  Based on this goal, the 
following land management activities will be allowed by the owner of the Property: 
 
A. 
Grazing of domestic livestock. 
 
B. 
Grazing and pasturing horses, cattle, mules, sheep, and llamas, provided that 
range conditions shall be maintained at, or improved from, those documented 
in the EDR.  Grazing of other livestock species shall be subject to prior 
approval by the Conservancy. 
 
C. 
The Parties acknowledge that given the topography, historic ownership and 
management of the Property, grazing management has occurred across the 
boundary between the states of Colorado and Wyoming.  Further, the Parties 
agree that grazing pastures on the Property shall be defined by existing fences, 
and not by Property ownership.  Properly managed grazing is expected to have 
no significant impact on the Conservation Values and in some instances is 
necessary to maintain said values.  In addition, grazing practices on the 
adjacent Red Mountain Open Space Property shall also occur in a manner such 
that it maintains range condition in a “good” or “excellent” condition as defined 
by the Natural Resources Conservation Service. 
 
D. 
Fence Maintenance and Repair.  The City shall be responsible for 
maintenance, repair, and reconstruction of existing fencing, as documented in 
the EDR, and for the construction of new fences, provided that boundary or 
pasture-division fences shall not unduly restrict or exclude wildlife use of the 
Property. Wildlife friendly fences are defined by research and various agencies. 
Specifications are available in various textbooks, agency publications, and from  
 
 
 
 
 

the Colorado State University (CSU) and University of Wyoming (UW) 
extension service and NRCS.  These fences facilitate the safe and 
uninterrupted movement of pronghorn, mule deer, and elk.  
 
E. 
Control of Noxious Weeds.  Use of agricultural chemicals for the following 
purposes and under the following conditions are permitted: 
 
1. 
For the control of noxious weeds, as required by Wyoming state law, 
and for the control of other invasive exotic plant species, provided 
that chemical herbicides may be used only in those amounts and 
with a frequency of application that constitute the minimum 
necessary for control, and that the herbicide is not applied by aerial 
spraying. 
 
2. 
For the control of agricultural or forest pests, subject to prior 
approval by the Conservancy.  
 
3. 
Use of biological weed and insect control agents is subject to prior 
approval by the Conservancy. 
 
4. 
For most noxious weeds, chemical treatment is the only effective or 
feasible means of control.  Therefore, chemicals should be allowed 
for use on the Property to treat noxious weeds when appropriate as 
long as used as directed on the labels and in consideration with the 
identified Conservation Values.  Eradication and control of 
noxious weeds are considered essential steps in successful 
conservation. 
 
F. 
Timber Harvest.  The selective harvesting of timber from the Property may be 
allowed but only for the purposes of controlling forest diseases, protecting 
persons or property from the hazards of falling trees or branches, reducing the 
risk of wildfire; and enhancing wildlife habitat.  These conditions apply to 
both living and standing dead trees. 
V. Miscellaneous Provisions 
 
11.  
Amendment: This Plan may be amended by the Parties at any time pursuant to a 
written instrument duly executed by authorized representatives of the Parties.  
 
12.  
The terms and conditions of this Cooperative Plan shall at all times be interpreted 
and implemented according to the overarching goals and objectives, terms and conditions of the 
Easement.  If there are any inconsistencies or conflicts between the provisions contained in this 
Cooperative Plan and the Easement, the terms of the Easement shall prevail.  
 
13.  
Five Year Reviews: This Plan shall be reviewed by the Parties at least once during 
the five (5) years following its execution for the purposes of updates and revisions and at least 
once during each additional five (5) year renewal term.  The Conservancy as holder of the 
 
 

Easement shall call all meetings necessary to review this Plan, set the agenda for each such meeting 
and act in the capacity of chair of the meeting.  In the absence of a call for a meeting by the 
Conservancy, each Party may, upon the five (5) year anniversary of the execution of this Plan, call 
for a meeting to review this Plan, which meeting shall be held at the earliest convenience of the 
Parties hereto.

of Cheyenne Street and Alley Department  
______________________________________________ 
Bid #E-2-23 was solicited for One (1) New 2022-2024 Patch Truck for the City of Cheyenne Street 
and Alley Department.   
Two (2) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  Each bidder submitted 
two (2) Bid Options, for a total of four (4) separate upfitter combinations. Of the four (4) submitted 
Bid Options, only one (1) Bidder fully met the Bid Specifications. 
The Bids were received from: 
1. Floyds Truck Center 
a. Option 1: Pro Patch Body 
 
i. Truck Specs – Fully Comply. 
 
ii. Body Specs, 3 Deviations/NO’s 
1. Main Body, 5, Left Blank. 
2. Heating System, 1, Marked No (Dry Radiant Heat, w/Dual 
Burners). 
3. Heating System, 2, Marked No. 
 
b. Option 2: Stepp Patch Body (RECOMMENDED FOR AWARD) 
 
i. Truck Specs – Fully Comply. 
ii. Body Specs – Fully Comply. 
 
2. McCandless Truck Center 
a. Option 1: Pro Patch Body 
 
i. Truck Specs – 2 Deviations/NO’s 
1. General Specifications, 15, Marked No, Spring Brake Modulator 
Valve 
2. Warranty, 1, Marked No. 
 
ii. Body Specs, 3 Deviations/NO’s 
1. Main Body, 5, Left Blank 

2. Heating System, 1, Marked No (Dry Radiant Heat, w/Dual 
Burners) 
3. Heating System, 2, Marked No 
 
b. Option 2: Stepp Patch Body 
i. Truck Specs – 2 Deviations/NO’s 
1. General Specifications, 15, Marked No, Spring Brake Modulator 
Valve 
2. Warranty, 1, Marked No. 
 
ii. Body Specs – Fully Comply 
 
The Bidding Documents contain a Surcharge and Escalator – Special Provision, that allows for  
the review of surcharges related to the following items: 
 
1. 
Original Equipment Manufacturer (OEM) and/or Body surcharges applied to the cost of 
the vehicles after the bid opening date. 
2. 
Model year escalators 
 
The Surcharge & Escalator Contingency, is for an amount not-to-exceed Fifteen Percent (15%), 
of the total value of  requested items on the Bid. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Vicki Nemecek (Public Works), Bryan Bell (Fleet Maintenance), and 
Bill Gonzales (Street & Alley), and their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid 
from Floyd’s Truck Center, with the Stepp Manufacturing Patch Body.   
 
The requested Bid award amount is $326,499.15, for One (1) New 2022-2024 Patch Truck, with 
selection of Additional Bid Items ABI 1. Extended warranty for engine and aftertreatment for 5 
years, ABI 3. Back up camera, ABI 4. Release Agent System, ABI 6. Patch Body Extended 
Warranty (Add 2 Additional Years), and the 15% Escalator & Surcharge Contingency. 
 
The City will not be accepting the Trade-In Offer on Block #5696, and that unit will be held for 
disposal via surplus auction.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
September 20, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #E-4-23 for Ice Control Product, for the City of Cheyenne 
Street & Alley Division  
______________________________________________ 
Bid #E-4-23 was solicited for Ice Control Product, for the City of Cheyenne Street & Alley 
Division. 
 
Three (3) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.   
 
The Bids were received from: 
1. Desert Mountain Corp.  Riverton, Wyoming. 
a. Submitted only responsive bid. 
 
2. Morton Salt.  Chicago, Illinois. 
a. Submitted a “No Bid”. 
 
3. Compass Minerals.  Overland Park, Kansas  
a. Submitted a “No Bid”. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Vicki Nemecek (Public Works), and Bill Gonzales (Street & Alley), 
and their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Desert Mountain Corp. 
 
The Bidding Documents contain a Fuel Surcharge Provision that allows for the escalation and de-
escalation of cost per ton, based on weekly fuel costs for the Rocky Mountain Region.  The Bid 
Award request, includes a 10% Contingency to cover the additional cost per ton, if fuel costs rise.  
The cost per ton has the potential to decrease as well, if fuel costs fall. 
 
Funding for this project, is requested in an amount not to exceed $891,000.00.  This cost is 
inclusive of the maximum of 3,000 Tons for 2022-2023, maximum of 3,000 Tons for 2023-2024, 
as well as inclusion of the 10% Fuel Surcharge Contingency over the Two (2) year period.

WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein,

ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Happy Jack Landfill Litter Fence Repair” project all in strict 
accordance with the Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: 
Addendum One (1), dated August 30, 2022, and Addendum Two (2), dated August 31, 2022. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by November 30, 2022.  If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, the sum of Thirty Nine 
Thousand Thirty Seven Dollars ($39,037.00).  In the event there are changes in the estimated 
quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities shall 
govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly.  The City agrees to pay the 
above amount for contractual services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate 
documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for percentage of estimated work 
completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable 
due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay 
By Stefanie Boster at 10:01 am, Sep 16, 2022

Page 2 of 2 
 
Request or the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the 
estimate for approval prior to payment. 
 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work completed 
for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement has 
been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and 
all Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; 
Affidavit of Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final 
payment will not be made until the above documents have been received by the 
City and all items on the Punch List have been completed, and the advertising 
requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. 
This Agreement;  
2. 
Addenda; 
3. 
Invitation for Bids; 
4. 
Instructions to Bidders; 
5. 
Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. 
General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. 
Supplemental Conditions; 
8. 
Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. 
Specifications and Special Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, 
forms the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has authorized 
the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the parties 
hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written. 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING

Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director 
Subject: Submission of 2021 Urban Renewal Authority Annual Report 
Date: 
September 26, 2022  
 
Background:  
 
The attached report provides a summary of the Urban Renewal Authority’s activities over the prior 
calendar year. The annual report details assets, liabilities, income, and operating expenses for 2021 and 
is required by Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-136 and Cheyenne Municipal Code § 2.80.050. 
 
This item will not be referred to committee.  
 
Attachments: 
1. 2021 Urban Renewal Authority Annual Report 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

Page  1 of 3 
 
Urban Renewal 
Authority 
 
2021  
Annual Report 

Page  2 of 3 
 
The purpose of this report is to provide a summary of the Urban Renewal Authority’s activities over the 
prior calendar year.  The annual report details assets, liabilities, income, and operating expenses for 
2021.  
 
The purpose of this report is to comply with Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-136 and Cheyenne Municipal  
Code § 2.80.050, Annual Reports.   
Purpose 
Background 
Urban renewal is a state-enabled economic development tool used to revitalize deteriorating or 
underdeveloped areas. Redevelopment of urban areas is often more expensive than new development, 
so public action through an Urban Renewal Authority can be used to encourage private investment in 
the area. The Wyoming Urban Renewal Code enables municipalities to set up Urban Renewal Authorities 
which have the ability to designate Urban Renewal Projects. These projects can use a variety of tools for 
redevelopment, including tax increment financing (TIF).   
 
The Urban Renewal Authority (URA) is comprised of a board of five members knowledgeable in 
community planning, urban renewal, and business management. The URA reviews Urban Renewal Plans 
and Projects for specific areas and makes recommendations regarding the use of Tax Increment 
Financing (TIF). The Urban Renewal Plans and Projects and TIF-related development agreements require 
approval by the Governing Body.  
 
The URA was formed on June 28, 2021, with the passage of Ordinance No. 4381.  On June 28, 2021, 
the Governing Body adopted Resolution No. 6156 determining that blighted areas in the community 
allowing the URA to execute powers under the State of Wyoming Urban Renewal Code to authorize  
rehabilitation and redevelopment of these defined areas as necessary for the public safety, health, 
morals, and welfare for the residents of the City of Cheyenne.  
 
 
Urban Renewal 
Authority 
 
2021  
Annual Report 

Page  3 of 3 
 
Plan and Projects 
 
Review and recommendation of the Hitching Post 
Urban Renewal Plan and Project.  The Plan and 
Project utilized the URA powers to assist with costs 
relating to infrastructure improvements, relocation of 
utilities, and the remediation of environmental 
hazards.  The intent of this plan was to assist in the 
redevelopment of a blighted hotel that had sat in 
disrepair for approximately ten years.   
 
Public Outreach 
 
Creation of Urban Renewal Authority Website, 
www.CheyenneCity.org/URA 
 
Assets 
 
The URA obtained no assets in calendar year 2021.   
 
Liabilities 
 
Through the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, 
the URA entered into a TIF Development agreement 
pledging up to on $1.2 million of tax increment 
revenue collected to remediation of environmental 
hazards associated with Swagger  Construction, LLC’s 
demolition and remediation of the Hitching Post.  The 
Development agreement limits reimbursement to 
collected tax increment funds or the 25-year time 
period of the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and 
Project  and extends no financial commitment to the 
City or URA.  
 
Income  
 
No revenue was been generated by the URA in 2021.   
 
Operating Expenses 
 
The URA is acting under the umbrella of the City in 
the Planning and Development Department’s Long 
Range Planning Division.  The City is absorbing all 
costs associated with the URA at this time.  
 
Financial Statement 
 
Revenue: $0 
Expenses: $0 
Savings/Checking accounts: Not established 
Summary of Activities

Please explain your interest in serving on this
As of young adult with a disability, I feel that each and
Board/Committee/Commission
every one of us deserves to live the best life possible.
To me, living the best life possible is dependent on
community involvement and memorable experiences.
In my experience, these memorable experiences
come from attending a Symphony concert at the
Cheyenne CMc Center, being able to take a walk in
your neighborhood, and being able to dine at local
restaurants. As an individual with a disability, there
have been several times where the walk in my
neighborhood was not enjoyable because there was
no curb cut out for me to access with my wheelchair.
Some restaurants I have been unable to access due to
lack of accessibility. Limited transportation makes
participating in community events difficult for those
who can’t drive, I have experienced this many times.
Cheyenne is a great community, however for those of
us with a disability sometimes it’s great attributes go
unseen. I feel that every Cheyenne resident should
have the opportunity to embrace our community to
the fullest by living and working among community
members. No resident with a disability should be
denied the opportunities the Cheyenne Community
presents. I believe that with my experience with living
with a disability in Cheyenne, I can bring valuable
knowledge to the improvements that need to be made
in order for Cheyenne residents to enjoy Cheyenne.
No resident in any Community should have to struggle
to enjoy their community and by serving on the
mayor’s Council for people with disabilities, I feel that
I can help in that struggle for Cheyenne community
members.
