# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-09-26_TotalExtraction_30280189.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
September 26, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Absent: Scott 
Roybal.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Mayor Collins announced the legal advertisement published on September 23, 2022 in the 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle related to the public hearing for a proposed trade of property between 
the City of Cheyenne and Mark Eisele contained a typographical date error and advised the public 
hearing will be held on October 10, 2022 as announced on the agenda. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Dr. Rinne and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Cook and Mr. Esquibel, item #17 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request 
by Mr. Cook and pursuant to support received by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson, item #18 was 
removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Dr. Aldrich and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Cook and Mr. Johnson, item #24(f) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request 
by Mr. White and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Esquibel, item #25(a) was 
removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Johnson moved to approve remaining items, seconded 
by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 12, 2022.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –EMERGENCY READING – Amending Section 10.08.010, State Law 
Adopted, of Chapter 10.08, State Traffic Laws Adopted, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the 
Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to update the incorporation of state motor 
vehicle statutes.  Mr. Cook moved to suspend the rules pursuant to State Statute §15-1-115(c), 
seconded by Dr. Aldrich. Motion to suspend the rules carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Mr. Cook moved to 
approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Comments were made by the 
following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. 
Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. White. (#4463) 
 
 
 

ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from HR High-Density Residential to CB 
Community Business for land located north of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range 
Boulevard and Moran Avenue.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne voting 
“no”.  Public comment was made by: Michael White, Cushing Terrell, on behalf of Les Schwab 
Tires.  Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich. 
(#4464) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MR Medium-Density Residential to NR-2 
Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density for land located south of 12th Street, east of Logan 
Avenue.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Public comment was made 
by: Kirk Johanson, property owner. (#4465) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 2.32.020, Department Personnel, of 
Chapter 2.32, Fire Department, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, to amend provision 
governing the appointment of Fire Department Personnel.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#4466) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 10.12.160, Authority to Impound 
Illegally Parked Vehicles, and Section 10.12.170, Immobilization, of Title 10, Vehicles and 
Traffic, modifying time period for vehicle impoundment after immobilization.  Mr. Cook moved 
to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. Cook moved to amend by substitute 
dated September 20, 2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson 
voting “no”.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Comments were made by 
the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis to CB 
Community Business for land north of Pershing Boulevard, east of College Drive.  Mr. Cook 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present.  Public comment was made by: Ben Hornok, Project 
Manager.  Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from Swan Ranch PUD Planned Unit Development 
(Ordinance #3989) to CB Community Business for land located north of High Plains Road, west 
of I-25.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion  
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. Public comment was made 
by: Shane Hansen, Steil Surveying. Comments were made by the following members of the 
governing body: Mayor Collins.  
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 9.08.020, Annoying, Obscene or 
Threatening Telephone Calls, of Chapter 9.08, Offenses Against Public Peace and Decency, of 
Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
modifying the elements of the offense of annoying, obscene or threatening telephone calls – 
referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Esquibel) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Reappropriating the Fiscal Year 2023 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16-4-112 & 16-4-113.  Reporting for Finance Committee, 
Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute 
dated September 13, 2022, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Main motion as amended by substitute 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Comments were made by the 
following members of the governing body: Mr. Cook.  (#6269) 
 
[CA]

RESOLUTION – Expressing the will of the Governing Body directing the Mayor to hire a

Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. White.  Comments were made by the

RESOLUTION – Expressing the will of the Governing Body to prioritize and adequately

RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Lake

RESOLUTION – Extending the City approval of an exception to the ‘201 Agreement’ for

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-05-02_TotalExtraction_63099685.pdf
  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 9, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich (via Zoom), Bryan Cook, Ken 
Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff 
White. Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Roybal and Mr. Cook, agenda item #’s 36(b), 36(c), 37(c) and 40(b) were removed from 
the Consent Agenda.   Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne 
voting “no”.  
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 25, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
      
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for 2600 Missile Drive (located east of 
I-25, north of Missile Drive). Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, provided 
information on the property, noting it is already partially in the city, and reviewed requirements of 
compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Lloyd 
advised the current development is a lumber company and the use will be expanded.  Jim Ridgeway 
inquired if the property location is adjacent to or on an abandoned city landfill.  Charles Bloom, 
Director of Planning and Development, explained that since the property is already developed, the 
location has no bearing on the annexation and if there is any contamination it is the landowner’s 
responsibility.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been 
met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for Saddle Ridge, 18th Filing (located 
northwest of the intersection of Christensen Road and Pershing Boulevard). Seth Lloyd, Planning 
and Development Department, provided information on the property, noting it is a continuation of 
the Saddle Ridge development and will include Pershing and Christensen rights-of-way and there 
will be no facilities on the site.  Mr. Lloyd further reviewed requirements of compliance with 
annexation conditions contained in state statutes.  There being no further comments or input and 
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 

 
Mayor Collins opened a public to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions 
required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for a portion of the S1/2 of Section 27, T.13N., 
R.67W, of the 6th PM, Laramie County, WY (located west of I-25, north of High Plains Road).  
Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, provided information on the property, noting 
the property will contain an entertainment facility, and reviewed requirements of compliance with 
annexation conditions contained in state statutes.  There being no further comments or input and 
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a bar and grill license application filed with the 
City Clerk’s Office for Billy Jacks – Wyoming LLC d/b/a Billy Jack’s Pizza Pub, 4020 Airport 
Parkway West Suite 1, Cheyenne, WY.  Jerry Cox, Director of Operations for Billy Jack’s Pizza 
Pub, advised he was available for questions. Upon inquiry, Mr. Cox explained the application is 
for their airport location and the application was submitted prior to consideration of the airport 
submitting an application pursuant to recently passed House Bill 0055 for a retail liquor license.  
Mr. Cox noted they have a meeting with the airport director later this week about submitting an 
application for a retail liquor license.  Support for the business at the airport was expressed.  Upon 
inquiry, Kris Jones, City Clerk, explained the retail liquor license must be applied for by the airport 
and the airport can contract with one entity for food and beverage services.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public 
hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a microbrewery license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Blue Raven Brewery, LLC, d/b/a Blue Raven Brewery, LLC, 209 E. 
18th St., Cheyenne, WY.  Richard Hart, Blue Raven Brewery, explained they are in the process of 
acquiring the current Danielmark’s Brewery and advised he was available for questions.  There 
being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins 
closed the public hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from AR Agricultural Residential to Harmony Valley 
Planned Unit Development (Ordinance #4274) for land located north of West College Drive and 
east of York Avenue.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body. (#4432)   
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 10.52.030, Parking Not to Obstruct 
Traffic, of Chapter 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and 
Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to postpone until May 23, 2022, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 9.12.080, Electric Fences Prohibited, 
of Chapter 9.12, Offenses Against Property, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of

the City of Cheyenne.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on second

seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mayor Collins explained the substitute defines the affected area as the 
DDA district.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.

RESOLUTION – Declaring that the Municipalities of Laramie County have agreed that 
the current State Statutes regarding the designation of liquor licenses issued by the governing 
bodies of said municipalities are stifling economic growth, and are requesting the State Legislative 
committees and State Legislature review, consider, and adopt modifications to state statutes 
regarding said licenses.  Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. White moved 
to amend by substitute dated May 3, 2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Mayor Collins advised the 
substitute includes the addition of “food trucks” in the seventh “Whereas” paragraph.  Motion to 
amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  It was noted that 
food trucks were added since they have become popular since liquor laws were originally written, 
and comment was made that the resolution was left vague to give the legislature flexibility.  Main 
motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
(#6231)

Designation of Official Voting Delegate and Alternate Delegate for the City of Cheyenne 
at the June 9, 2022 Wyoming Association of Municipalities Summer Convention Business 
Meeting to be held in Laramie, Wyoming.  Mr. White moved to approve Councilman Dr. Mark 
Rinne as official voting delegate and Mayor Patrick Collins as alternate voting delegate, seconded 
by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held May 23, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting, 
for a bar & grill liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Westby Edge LLC 
d/b/a Westby Edge LLC, 714 W. 20th St., Cheyenne, WY. 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held May 23, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting, 

for a microbrewery liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Westby Edge 
LLC d/b/a Westby Edge LLC, 714 W. 20th St., Cheyenne, WY. 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held May 23, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting, 
to receive comments and input for the Hynds and the Hole Urban Renewal Plan and Project as 
required by Wyoming Statute pursuant to Wyoming Statute §15-9-109.   
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held May 23, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting, 
to receive comments and input for the 15th Street Corridor Renewal Plan and Project as required 
by Wyoming Statute pursuant to Wyoming Statute §15-9-109.   
Announcement was made that a Calcutta, benefitting the Black Dog Animal Rescue, will 
be held Friday, June 3, 2022, beginning at 6:00 p.m. at Alf’s Pub and Bar, 1622 E. 19th Street, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming in conjunction with the 2022 Black Dog Animal Rescue Cornhole 
Tournament (35% of proceeds go to the Black Dog Animal Rescue).   
 
Under other business:   
 
 
Mr. Johnson provided information on the Rooted in Cheyenne planting event to be held on 
Saturday, May 14, 2022 and applauded Community Recreation and Events staff for the Friday’s 
on the Plaza concert announcement event.   
 
 
Mr. Segrave provided information on the Downtown Cleanup event to be held Saturday, 
May 14, 2022 starting at 10 a.m., noting another cleanup will be done before Cheyenne Frontier 
Days. 
 
 
Mr. Roybal moved to meet in executive session pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16-4-
405(a)(iii)(vii), seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  Mayor Collins announced members would meet in executive session, not open 
to the public; requested the City Attorney, City Clerk, City Treasurer, Chief of Staff and members 
of Planning and Development staff be in attendance and stated there would be no action taken by 
the governing body as a result of the executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting 
would officially conclude at the close of the executive session. (Clerk’s Note: executive session 
convened at 6:42 p.m.) 
 
 
Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 7:13 p.m. 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:13 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
May 19, 2022

Dear Mayor and Members of the City Council,
Please accept this letter as our introduction and cover letter for our application for a
brewery and bar and grill permit for the City of Cheyenne. It has been a pleasure working with
you all during the rezoning, platting, building and liquor license portion of this project and we
would like to thank you for your assistance in that endeavor. As most of you know, Dam
and I
are renovating a 90+ year old warehouse on the West Edge into a multi-use property consistent
with the City’s West Edge Plan. The warehouse, when completed, will have a brewery/bar and
grill with a large outdoor patio and game space, an event/overflow space and an apartment. We
are looking forward to incorporating our outdoor space with the Reed Avenue Corridor Project
and contributing to the revitalization of the West Edge.
We look forward to working with the City on this application and permit process and we
are happy to answer any questions or provide any additional information you may require.
Melissa (Misha) Westby

PROPOSED ALCOHOL TRAINING PROGRAM FOR WESTBY EDGE
Introductory statement:
We take this issue very seriously, we will provide consistent and substantive training for
all employees (and volunteers if any) serving alcohol in our establishment.
Initial orientation:
We will provide an initial orientation to all employees (and volunteers if any) when they
begin work about our commitment to a safe and secure establishment where alcohol will be served.
We will explain the required training including requirements of successfully completing the
training and updating and maintaining certifications.
Substantive training:
We will require that all employees (and volunteers if any) who sell, serve or deliver
alcoholic or malt beverages or provide on-site security are TIPS trained in a program approved by
the Wyoming Liquor Division by a certified trainer. Completion of the training will be a
requirement before the employee with be allowed to sell, serve or deliver alcohol. We will maintain
TIPS training and certification as part of our business records and make sure that every person
selling, serving, or delivering alcohol or involved in on-site security is current on his/her TIPS
training. This documentation will be available to law enforcement personnel for inspection.
Alcohol Related Policies:
No alcohol will be sold or served to persons under the legal drinking age within the licensed
or permitted building or authorized areas of service.
All incidents requiring law enforcement responsive services will be immediately reported
by the licensee, permittee, employees, volunteers, on-site managers, agents or any persons
providing on-site security services. Immediate notice will be provided to the Police Department of
any criminal or illegal activity observed on the licensee’s or permittee’ s property or adjoining
property affiliated with the sale or consumption of alcoholic liquor and malt beverages, including
the reporting of any assault, aggravated assault, or assault and battery.

WESTBY EDGE LLC
714 W. 20th Street
Cheyenne, WY 82001
April 21, 2022
The Honorable
Patrick Collins
Mayor of Cheyenne
The Honorable
Pete Laybourn
Scott Roybal
Jeff White
Bryan Cook
Dr. Mark Rinne
Tom Segrave
Dr. Michelle Aldrich
Richard Johnson
Ken Esquibel
City Council Members
2101 O’Neil Avenue
Cheyenne, Wyoming, 82001
Dear Mayor and Members of the City Council,
Please accept this letter as our introduction and cover letter for our application for a
brewery and bar and grill permit for the City of Cheyenne. It has been a pleasure working with
you all during the rezoning, platting, building and liquor license portion of this project and we
would like to thank you for your assistance in that endeavor. As most of you know, Darin and I
are renovating a 90+ year old warehouse on the West Edge into a multi-use property consistent
with the City’s West Edge Plan. The warehouse, when completed, will have a brewery/bar and
grill with a large outdoor patio and game space, an event/overflow space and an apartment. We
are looking forward to incorporating our outdoor space with the Reed Avenue Corridor Project
and contributing to the revitalization of the West Edge.
We look forward to working with the City on this application and permit process and we
are happy to answer any questions or provide any additional information you may require.
7
Melissa (Misha) Westby

PROPOSED ALCOHOL TRAINING PROGRAM FOR WESTBY EDGE
Introductory statement:
We take this issue very seriously, we will provide consistent and substantive training for
all employees (and volunteers if any) serving alcohol in our establishment.
Initial orientation:
We will provide an initial orientation to all employees (and volunteers if any) when they
begin work about our commitment to a safe and secure establishment where alcohol will be served.
We will explain the required training including requirements of successfully completing the
training and updating and maintaining certifications.
Substantive training:
We will require that all employees (and volunteers if any) who sell, serve or deliver
alcoholic or malt beverages or provide on-site security are TIPS trained in a program approved by
the Wyoming Liquor Division by a certified trainer. Completion of the training will be a
requirement before the employee with be allowed to sell, serve or deliver alcohol. We will maintain
TIPS training and certification as part of our business records and make sure that every person
selling, serving, or delivering alcohol or involved in on-site security is current on his/her TIPS
training. This documentation will be available to law enforcement personnel for inspection.
Alcohol Related Policies:
No alcohol will be sold or served to persons under the legal drinking age within the licensed
or permitted building or authorized areas of service.
All incidents requiring law enforcement responsive services will be immediately reported
by the licensee, permittee, employees, volunteers, on-site managers, agents or any persons
providing on-site security services. Immediate notice will be provided to the Police Department of
any criminal or illegal activity observed on the licensee’s or permittee’s property or adjoining
property affiliated with the sale or consumption of alcoholic liquor and malt beverages, including
the reporting of any assault, aggravated assault, or assault and battery.

The brewery premises consists of one building located on
north Property line and approximately 150 feet from the Ree
Avenue property line. The building is a metal sided wood
framed warehouse with a poured concrete floor. The brewer
area consists of the northern portion of the building and it is
approximately 91.5’ X 65’. The building has standard windov
on the East wall and garage doors on the West and North
walls. The garage doors lead to the entry way on the West
side and the alley on the North Side. There are two man
doors one on the West side and one on the East side.
The West facing half of the brewery premises from the
entryway on the East wall on the South side and from the
partitioned brewery equipment area will be used as the tavet
pub. This space is the full 91.5’ on the West side of the
building going back approximately 35’.
The East side of the brewery premises, the brewery
equipment including tanks, the kitchen and the built in coolei
(including the holding tanks) will not be accessible to the
public and will be appropriately secured.
The brewery equipment area will be secured with a locking
garage door and a locking mandoor. The cooler will be
secured by a locking cooler door. The kitchen will be
separated by the bar and a sign.
We will be using a 7bbl brewing system and plan to brew 4 V
6 times per month. We have two 7 bbl fermenters and 2 7 b
brite tanks. we have 4 8bbl holding tanks in the cooler which
will use to serve beer directly from. The holding tanks will be
calibrated and are equiped with appropriate measuring
devises. We will transfer the beer from the brite tanks to the
holding tanks prior to any sales and we will measure the beE
when we transfer to the holding tanks and keep accurate an
timely records to determine tax due.

ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 10.52.030, PARKING NOT TO 
OBSTRUCT TRAFFIC, OF CHAPTER 10.52, STOPPING, STANDING 
AND PARKING GENERALLY, OF TITLE 10, VEHICLES AND 
TRAFFIC, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING. 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
10.52.030  
Parking not to obstruct traffic. 
 
No person shall park a recreational vehicle or trailer as defined in Section 10.52.200 of this 
chapter, vehicle upon a street, other than an alley, in such a manner or under such conditions as to 
leave available less than ten (10) feet of the width of the roadway, as measured from the centerline, 
or centerline pavement marking, for free movement of vehicular traffic.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-05-02_TotalExtraction_45096883.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. __________ 
 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 10.52.230 DOWNTOWN 
CONSTRUCTION/MAINTENANCE 
WORKER 
PARKING 
IN 
AN 
ESTABLISHED TIMED PARKING ZONE, OF CHAPTER 10.52, 
STOPPING, STANDING AND PARKING GENERALLY, OF TITLE 10, 
VEHICLES AND TRAFFIC, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING.” 
 
 
BE IT HEREBY ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That Section 10.52.230, Downtown Construction/Maintenance Worker 
Parking in an Established Timed Parking Zone, of Chapter 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking 
Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is 
hereby amended as set out in this ordinance.  Sections currently existing in Chapter 10.52 not set 
out within this ordinance remain unchanged.  
 
10.52.230 
DOWNTOWN CONSTRUCTION/MAINTENANCE WORKER PARKING 
IN AN ESTABLISHED TIMED PARKING ZONE. 
 
A. 
The city traffic engineer or their designee is authorized to issue special purpose 
monthly on-street parking permits for bona fide downtown construction/maintenance work.  
1. 
Such permit will allow construction/maintenance workers to park in a 
single parking place in excess of the signed two-hour limitation.  
2. 
Applications for special purpose monthly on-street downtown construction 
worker parking permits shall be submitted with the police department parking administration 
manager.  
3. 
Permits will be issued at a cost to the applicant of sixty dollars ($60.00) 
per vehicle per month.  
4. 
Permits may be revoked by the police department parking administration 
manager for the following reasons:  
a. 
Nonpayment for two billing cycles; or  
b. 
Unlawful use of the permit.  
5. 
For purposes of this section, "downtown" shall mean the Downtown 
Development Authority defined District. 
6.  Specifically exempt from this ordinance is right-of-way construction activities 
approved by the City of Cheyenne.    
 
By Stefanie Boster at 6:04 pm, May 02, 2022

Section 2. 
This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real

(b) 
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as 
Exhibit A, said map prepared by Jeffrey B. Jones, a Professional Land Surveyor, 
licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 5910; and 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; and 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, 
be furnished; and 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned A1 
Agricultural and Rural Residential by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and 
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 
entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2. 
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
 

Section 3. 
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4. 
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5. 
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00098-01: Annexation of Saddle Ridge, 18th Filing
REQUEST:
An annexation of 55.3 acres of land
LOCATION:
Northwest of the intersection of Christensen Road and Pershing Boulevard
APPLICANT:
WJE, LLC
AGENT:
WJE, LLC
John Sayers
OWNER:
WJE, LLC
PURPOSE:
To develop a large area of currently vacant land with urban residential 
development
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II Development
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00096
May 9, 2022
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing of 55.3 acres of land to develop land with urban-residential style 
development. Currently, the property is one ownership parcel in the County. The land is currently 
undeveloped. A zone change and plat is proposed concurrently with this annexation. All applications are 
anticipated to come to final consideration before the Governing Body at the same meeting.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by a submission 
to change the zoning from County A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential to the City MR Medium-density 
Residential zone. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential in the County. The property is 
concurrently proposed to be annexed and rezoned to the City MR Medium-density Residential zone. The 
City MR zone is intended for The MR District is intended for a mix of density and dwelling types within a 
neighborhood. The development pattern is primarily residential and requires full access to City services. 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the future 
occupants. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and

UDC-22-00096
May 9, 2022
sanitation services. The property, if occupied, will also be required to be connected to City 
sanitary sewer instead of having septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The property is adjacent to previously annexed areas which are currently developing. 
The contiguity and the proximity to surrounding urban-style development makes the parcel a 
natural geographic part of the City. The development of this property will benefit those who live 
within the City and will be a part of the social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. Utilities already exist in the area. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is about 52% contiguous, along the western and 
southwestern edges.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
 
 
 
 
 
 

UDC-22-00096
May 9, 2022
6)
The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on April 7, 2022. The public 
hearing for this annexation is scheduled for May 9, 2022. Twenty (20) business days prior to the 
announced public hearing is April 11, 2022. The earliest confirmation of delivery was dated April 
8, 2022. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
Policies within PlanCheyenne encourage responsible growth. As this annexation is on the edge of the 
community, any development action needs to take into careful consideration how to extend 
infrastructure, including utilities and streets. 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with a mix of urban residential uses. These areas are expected to develop with 
full access to urban services  such areas are best served by being within City limits. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, the following comments were received: 
- 
New septic systems will not be allowed. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to be extended to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property.

2. Submitted Application 
3. Applicant-submitted annexation map
